# Additive tuning of 2026-10-24, skills round 1 variant 3, scenario 4

## Run identity

```yaml
model: claude-sonnet-5
served_version: claude-sonnet-5
tree: sha256:bc9ff145ae7bc2a53ece57ca1442ff3d5ef1528cca5641cbfc877419836a844a
lock: sha256:7b9b260a20cfe3c7b36337353499537aa39963f4be464ebcc9f28e55773eef36
selection: sha256:8d4747a05d1213146c97d37a574a457ce8aeee431a3f9f6a9c67c79021d1f437
read_set: sha256:f18e4fd7eafa09aabd6204d8a41112a4af8892eb03c0b5d7fb9d3bf5f927b3a3
seed: 0
harness: 0.6.1
tier: additive
arm: scenario-4
at: 2026-10-24
cost_cents: 2247
```

**This line is the `screen 3` stage of round 1 of the skills layer in the tuning round of the additive campaign, which [#1777](https://github.com/headwater-ai/headwater/issues/1777) asked for.** A screen measures one variant on a small batch, so that the round can rank the variants before it pays for a confirmation. Variant 3 is one commit, `88bea5de`, on `bea46653`, the commit of [#1855](https://github.com/headwater-ai/headwater/pull/1855), with the tool fix of [#1857](https://github.com/headwater-ai/headwater/pull/1857). It was screened from the pushed branch `tune/skills-r1-v3-pin`, because #1857 lets a pin be on a branch under `origin/tune/`. `tools/probe/try.sh launch` ran it from the one-line spec `additive scenario-4 tuning`. It is the `scenario-4` arm of the additive tier over 5 probes of the tuning set, at 5 repetitions per probe, on `claude-sonnet-5` under Claude Code 2.1.288.

**What the variant changes.** The commit makes `headwater-authoring` the gate that a task passes before it is complete. It also adds a common "Run the engine" section to the skills. The shipped skill bodies call `headwater`, and `headwater` is not on the `PATH` of a session, so without that section a session cannot run the command that a skill names.

**One tree, one pin, one launch.** Every session ran in a fresh copy of a `git archive` of the pinned tree, and the engine of that tree. The pin was `88bea5de` for the whole batch. The canary of 8 sessions was read before `canary-passed` was written. Every flagged record stated that the session stayed in its workspace, that no fetch request was refused, and that no host file was readable at the outside paths it named. It did not launch the batch again. The `tree` digest in the identity above is the digest the assembly recorded.

**What the recorder counted.** The line holds 25 graded sessions and cost 2247 cents: the intent hook live in 0, 0 stopped at the turn cap, 0 stopped at the session budget, 18 paths outside the workspace named, 0 sessions uncounted, 0 web-tool calls. A session the recorder could not count adds nothing to either count, so each count is a floor.

**The result.** Variant 3 passed 15 of 25 graded sessions, and 10 of 25 sessions used the skills layer. The base row compares this measurement with the base of scenario 3, which passed 76 of 145 in [the base transcript](additive-tuning-of-2026-10-24-base-re-measure-of-scenario-3.md). The difference is +7.6 points, with the interval [-13.3, +26.0]. The interval includes zero, so this screen shows no gain over scenario 3. A screen is never kept, and only a confirmation is (owner, 2026-10-22, #1777).

**Where the variant ranks.** The round ranks its screens on skill use first and on pass rate second. The best so far used the skills in 37 of 145 sessions, in [the best-so-far transcript](additive-tuning-of-2026-10-24-skills-round-1-best-so-far-scenario-4.md). The four screens rank in this order: variant 1 (23 used, 21 passed), variant 4 (21 used, 20 passed), variant 2 (16 used, 19 passed) and variant 3 (10 used, 15 passed). Variant 3 ranks fourth. Variant 1 ranks first, so it goes on to the confirmation of round 1 at 145 sessions, and this variant does not.

**The contract of this batch.** The sessions ran under the recorder contract of spec 15: a `bwrap` sandbox with no network of its own, a configuration directory that holds a copy of the credentials and nothing else, the permission mode `dontAsk`, and `WebSearch` and `WebFetch` denied. The owner approved $1,512.00 for the whole tuning round, over every attempt and every layer. The driver ran this try with a cap of 12250 cents and a session budget of 300 cents.

**What the batch cost.** The driver charged 2247 cents against its cap, and the 25 graded sessions account for all of it. The 2247 cents go against the tuning ceiling of 129000 cents, and not against the base ceiling of 22000 cents that [#1833](https://github.com/headwater-ai/headwater/issues/1833) gave to the bases.

**Where the raw recordings are.** The raw session logs are outside git, under `/mnt/build/hw-1777/skills-r1-v3/a/sessions/L1-*/raw.jsonl` on the host that ran the batch. Nobody deletes them until the report of [#1779](https://github.com/headwater-ai/headwater/issues/1779) is published.

**Where the other figures are.** The rate and the interval of each probe are in the generated result that reads this transcript.

The default is 5.\\\" to \\\"7\\\".\\n\\nThe rest of the route report (anchored/named pointer ordering, withheld-count wording, evidence format, etc.) is intentionally unchanged — only the default count moves from 5 to 7.\\n\\nI know of one more file that mentions the old number in a way that looks like it could now be stale or wrong: docs/reviews/the-sixty-four-restored-help-strings-checked-against-the-binary.md, row 10 of its table, which records a historical check of the `route --budget` help string and says \\\"Five by default\\\" plus observed output \\\"the default offered 5 pointers and printed `the budget withheld 21 more pointers`\\\" etc. I'm unsure whether this is a frozen historical record of a past observation (which should stay as written) or a live assertion that must track current binary behavior.\\n\\nPlease report, across the whole corpus (docs/, .headwater/, engine/crates, integrations/, tools/):\\n- Every governed document whose content is now stale or inconsistent because of this change (not just files that mention \\\"five\\\" generically — the task is specifically about the route command's default pointer budget/count).\\n- For docs/reviews/the-sixty-four-restored-help-strings-checked-against-the-binary.md specifically: is it the kind of document that should be updated to match current behavior, or a frozen record of a past test run that should stay as written with the discrepancy noted some other way (e.g. a new obligation)? Tell me which governance rule or decision record settles that.\\n- Any test fixtures, decision records, or obligation records that assert or rely on the literal number 5/five for route's default budget and would now be factually wrong or inconsistent.\\n- Any front-matter fields (e.g. `status_since`, `last_verified`, version) on docs/interfaces/headwater-route.md that this kind of content change obligates me to bump, per this repo's own rules.\\n\\nReport back a concrete list of file paths with what's wrong in each and what governance rule says so, not a full rewrite — I will make the edits myself.\"}"

    - tool: "Agent"
      argument: "{\"description\":\"Find stale docs after route budget default change\",\"subagent_type\":\"headwater-maintainer\",\"prompt\":\"In this headwater repository, I changed `headwater route`'s default pointer budget from 5 to 7 (it was always meant to stay a \\\"ranked pointers\\\" default, nothing else about the report format changed). Edits made so far:\\n\\n- engine/crates/query/src/route.rs: `Budget::default()` now returns `Budget { pointers: 7 }`, and its doc comment above the struct now says \\\"The default is seven\\\" instead of \\\"The default is five.\\\"\\n- engine/crates/cli/src/lib.rs: the `--budget` arg's clap help string for the `Route` variant now says \\\"Seven by default\\\" instead of \\\"Five by default\\\".\\n- docs/interfaces/headwater-route.md: \\\"The default budget is five ranked pointers.\\\" -> \\\"...seven ranked pointers.\\\", and the options table row \\\"The default is 5.\\\" -> \\\"The default is 7.\\\"\\n- integrations/vscode/test/client.test.js and integrations/jetbrains/test/ClientTest.java: comments and assertions that capped `routed.pointers.length`/`.size()` at 5 now cap at 7 (both had a comment \\\"the engine answers at most five pointers\\\" and an assert message \\\"route answered past the default budget of 5\\\").\\n\\nI have NOT yet touched: engine/crates/query/tests/reads.rs (which has a doc comment on `a_task_that_matches_several_purposes_is_offered_more_than_one_kind` describing \\\"the default five hold more than one kind and more than one purpose\\\" — this describes current behavior, not just history before HW-DR-0070, so it may need rewording to \\\"seven\\\"), any blessed/recorded route fixtures under fixtures/ directories (query crate or elsewhere) that might record exact pointer counts or exact pointer lists produced under `Budget::default()`, docs/decisions/0070 and 0071 (which narrate specific test routes and counts under \\\"the default budget\\\" — I believe these are historical/frozen decision records and should NOT be rewritten, but confirm this against this repo's own doctrine on decision records), and docs/process/obligations/0224 and docs/evaluations/* that mention \\\"a default budget of five\\\" as a historical measurement (likely also frozen/historical).\\n\\nPlease tell me, per this repository's own governance rules:\\n1. Which currently-governed documents (by `governs` edges) cover engine/crates/query/src/route.rs, engine/crates/cli/src/lib.rs, or engine/crates/cli/src/main.rs, to confirm I haven't missed an editable governed doc besides docs/interfaces/headwater-route.md.\\n2. Whether any blessed/recorded test fixture (e.g. under engine/crates/query/fixtures/ or engine/crates/cli/tests fixtures) bakes in byte-exact route output that depends on the pointer count of 5, and so needs `HEADWATER_BLESS=1 cargo test ...` regeneration.\\n3. Whether docs/decisions/0070 and 0071, or docs/process/obligations/0224, or any docs/evaluations file, are governed/historical records this repo's doctrine says must stay as originally written (so they're correctly left alone), or whether any of them is actually a *living* document I should update.\\n4. Whether changing this default number itself is the kind of change that this repo's doctrine requires a NEW decision record for (separate from editing the interface doc), and if so, point me to the decision-record authoring doctrine that would govern writing it.\\n5. Any other file your tools can find (CI fixtures, census fixtures, graph fixtures, other language-client docs) that literally hardcodes the number 5 as this route budget default and that I should also update.\\n\\nReport findings as a concise list grouped by the five questions above, under 400 words. I will make any further edits myself — you are reporting/proposing only.\"}"
